# WHU validity phase: deviations log

Kept from the plan's freeze (`b4cf31a`, `validity_plan.md`): every departure from the plan or an incident in running
it, with its date, what happened, why, and whether it was decided or found before or after any check result was seen.
One row per entry, newest last. Dates are UTC.

| Date | What | Why | Before or after any check result was seen |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-30 | **Incident: study content sent to Google under a free-tier key.** Before any generate call, the runners sent text to Google's `count_tokens` endpoint (`models.count_tokens`, model `gemini-4-argon`) with the `GEMINI_API_KEY` then in `backend/.env`, which was on Google's Free Tier (found when every generate call was refused: 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, quotaId `GenerateContentInputTokensPerModelPerDay-FreeTier`; no generate call succeeded and $0 was spent). Sent, between about 16:55 and 17:35 UTC: (1) check 1's estimate, run twice (once by `judge_google.py --estimate`, once by the started run before its first generate call): the full M1 prompts for the 217 judgeable round-1 items (the frozen M1 v3 prompt with each cited source's held text, chunked as judge_m1 delivers it, and the drawn sentence with its context), about 8.3M tokens by Google's count, and the 91 M2 prompts (each blinded round-1 answer with one round-1 key conclusion), about 0.74M tokens, once; (2) check 4's estimate (`keys2.py prepare`): the six key source sets as they would be given to the drafter (WHU-Q1-01 to -04, WHU-S01, WHU-S02: Europe PMC full texts and IPCC report text), about 1.19M tokens. Google's terms for the unpaid tier may allow it to use submitted content to improve its products; whether that covers `count_tokens` is not known. **It cannot be recalled.** No barred-publisher text is held in these sets (round 1's snapshots and the keys' sources exclude Elsevier, Wiley, SAGE and NCCN copies without a CC licence); Claude Q1-04's answer, which used a Gmail connector, was screened before judging and holds no personal content (round1_deviations, 2026-10-29). Also sent before then, no study content: `models.list` and `models.get` (metadata). | The runners called `count_tokens` for estimates without first checking that the key was on a paid tier; the session assumed a priced model meant a paid account. Remedy (advisor ruling, this date): no Google call of any kind until a preflight confirms a paid tier (`judge_google.preflight`), and Google estimates from a local approximation. Fred turned billing on the same day. | Found before any check result was seen (no Google reply exists; checks 9 and 6, $0, had run and use no Google call). |

| 2026-10-30 | **Check 5b withdrawn as a correction instrument; it decides nothing (advisor ruling; the advisor's error, recorded as such).** Reason, independent of the result's direction: FIGURE_NEAR was used as a "supported" reference, but the judges' fails on those items are framing labels (Overstates, Wrong source, Absent) that a figure check cannot see, so the reference was invalid by construction; and with 1 FIGURE_ABSENT item in 177, the judges' sensitivity cannot be estimated, so the §5 correction degenerates (it falls back to the reference share). The conclusion-change rule does not apply to 5b. **The full output stays in the record** (`results/check5b.json`, `results/check5b_classes.json`), including the result the rule had flagged: WHU vs Gemini, corrected difference on figure claims +0.08 (95% CI 0.00 to 0.21), on 3 judged Gemini figure items. **5b is reported only as a description** (mechanical figure check; not a human review; not a correction), led by the line (advisor, 2026-10-30): "113 of 177 figure claims (64%) carry no citation." The advisor's draft wording, "in 176 of 177 figure claims across all systems, the figure appears in the cited source", does not match the record and is corrected here, by derivation from `check5b_classes.json`: of 177 round-1 M1 items with a figure, 113 are uncited (no cited source to look in) and 1 has no cited source held; of the other 63, every claimed figure appears in the cited source for 57 (52 FIGURE_NEAR, 5 AMBIGUOUS with all figures found but not near two content words), some for 5, none exactly for 1 (the one FIGURE_ABSENT). On the 52 FIGURE_NEAR items the two judges gave 20 fail labels: Absent 8, Overstates 7, Wrong source 4, Contradicted 1: failures of what the sentence says beyond the figure, not invented numbers. §5 stands as ruled: no human check in this phase. | The advisor's ruling. | After 5b's result was seen; before checks 1-4 and 10 ran. |

| 2026-10-31 | **Blinding leak found (reported, not acted on; awaits a ruling).** WHU's page furniture survived blinding. Searched (case-insensitive) every blinded file of round 1 (`blinded/<q>/*.blind.txt`, `*.sentences.jsonl`, `*.adapted.md`, the WHU exports) and the 19 M2 answer files for: "Judged not material" (incl. "… to this question"), "Findings in our sources we judged outside this question" (a section heading), "outside this question", "we did not search", "we did not read", "This answer draws on", and the brand ("What Holds Up", "WHU", "whatholdsup"). The phrase list came from 4-word phrases present in at least 3 of the 4 WHU answers and in no other system's. **Brand: 0 occurrences in any blinded text.** **Furniture: in all 4 WHU blind texts and all 4 WHU M2 answers (A-8e796bb05e, A-6006bb18b3, A-1afd0594f1, A-8816086bcc), and in no other system's file.** "Judged not material" occurs 2 / 5 / 13 / 20 times (Q1-01 / -02 / -03 / -04). Sentences: 73 of 366 WHU sentences (4 / 7 / 31 / 31) carry a furniture phrase in the sentence and 69 in its context. **Scored M1 items: 12 of the 80 WHU items (of 371 in all)** carry furniture, 11 in the claim and 1 in the context only; 7 are cited and were judged (first read, both judges; 1 also in the repeat; 4 also in check 10), 5 are uncited (never judged). **Check 3's 20 WHU sentences included 4 of them** (X003, X048, X060, X091), and the guessers named that furniture as their clue. Rounds that used these files: round-1 M1 first read and repeat, round-1 M2 first read and repeat, check 10, check 3; check 1 (Google) and check 4's M2 rescoring would use them. Nothing was re-blinded or re-judged. | Found by check 3's clues. | After checks 3 and 10 results were seen; before checks 1, 2, 3b and 4. |

The 1500 therefore bounded something smaller than the summed count, consistent with a per-iteration bound; that last point is inferred, not documented. The replay rehearsal (orch/replay_rehearsal.py, `orchestrator.py rehearse <recorded slug> --replay`): the recorded run's held documents are served at their own URLs (its private acquire checkpoint's library index, bytes from the trial library, seeded into ~/Projects/whu-study-private/replay_http/<slug>/, which also keeps what the replay fetches live, $0 registries); replies are taken raw from the store where one exists (none for 02z-02zd), else PARSED from the run's own records where a record holds the whole answer (premise rows from premise.json, discovery from proposals.json, each belief-outlet round from rulings/premise_checks.jsonl), else synthetic (the rehearsal's generators: coverage, judgements, the premise reading -- its quotations are stored as offsets only --, the audit, rulings, gate roles, Google verdicts); every reply is logged with its source in events.jsonl. Each reply carries the recorded run's own output count for that call (the ledger row of the same step and order) and comes back stop_reason max_tokens when that count is at or over today's limit, so the doubled re-ask is exercised; a recorded reply that was itself cut off (02zd's premise reading, its run record's cut_off reply) is censored and replayed synthetic. The gate (orch/replay_gate.py run|check): replays 02z, 02za, 02zc and 02zd; PASS on Ready., on 'Insufficient sources: ...', or Not Ready by design at the recorded run's last step or later with no new STOP row (code_preflight's stops, keyed by their first label words); FAIL on an exception, an unrecognised prompt, or an earlier stop without an outcome; an outage is INCONCLUSIVE (fails; run again). The record is ~/Projects/whu-study-private/replay_gate/<code sha>.json (sha over every .py under whatholdsup/, factcheck_draft.py, spend_ledger.py, provider_retry.py); `orchestrator.py run --mode paid` refuses without a passing record for the current code. A trial replay of 02zd (before this commit's errata fix) reached Ready on 02zd's own documents: S001 and S003 held (S003 under ruling 5, its three bound claims noted), S004's landing route tried and its PDF refused on the same assessment-summary form rule (P132's caveat, now observed), premise / discovery / both belief rounds parsed, 81 replies synthetic. FOUND BY THAT REPLAY and fixed here: errata.preflight_rows listed a source whose correction we could NOT read under 'corrections obtained and read' (P133 counted it as accounted for); it is now listed only under 'corrections we could not read'. | tests/test_whu_replay_gate.py (23); orchestrator, reply-limit, policy-discovery and correction tests 219 passed; the gate itself runs at step 7 |

Running coverage once, after draft, bind, judgements, belief and premise_reading and before corrections_3b and commit_3b (the first full draft), puts every unit of every source relied on at that point to the model in one step; a second coverage pass for new units would add a second step to the chain, the checkpoints and the resume rules for the same work, and would still leave the order question for any later step. Coverage's own 'state' sentences bind to sources already relied on, so it creates no new relied-on source; an assessment unit its binding creates is that binding's own unit (used). Nothing coverage writes is read by judgements, belief or premise_reading (each reads the held texts and the premise), so moving it changes none of them. Steps after commit_3b that add bindings (omissions, audit) are where they were. Test: tests/test_whu_coverage_order.py (coverage after premise_reading, judgements and draft, before commit_3b; a source named only in what_the_sources_show is relied on). (2) Failed audit read (FAILED_READ): the sentence is withheld the same way; no human_reads.jsonl. (3) Unusable omission ruling reply: DECLARED_UNRULED, the readers'-list line 'Not added: “...” (n) (our ruling on it could not be completed).', counted against the declarations budget (coverage_check.COUNTED). (4) Refused OUT_OF_SCOPE element: the declaration stands as OUT_OF_SCOPE with no element and counts against the budget (never exempt). (5) Declarations over budget: OVER_DECLARATIONS_BUDGET, an honest stop: the row 'omission objections ruled' STOPs with 'Not Ready', and orchestrator.code_apply ends the run at the omissions step before any later step spends. (6) NOT_MATERIAL on a SERIOUS objection that CHANGES the conclusion: not accepted from the machine; the body sentence it is about is withdrawn by the lean fail-safe (CONCLUSION_WITHDRAWN; nothing declared, the advocate item not closed); if that sentence cannot be withdrawn (protected, code-written, a required line or off the page) the page is Not Ready (OVER_DECLARATIONS_BUDGET, the reason recorded). findings.py 'attested' needs nothing: under lean a licence-barred source is refused at acquire and a gate finding about its claim is ruled by ruling.py gate like any other. person_steps.md updated: none remains, nothing could not be automated. orch/writes.py: audit_stage.py's command now includes lean_failsafe (it writes rulings/lean_removed.jsonl).
